data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&quot;</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#C3E88D">21m00Tcm4TlvDq8ikWAM</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&quot;</span><span data-fern-style="color:#212121;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">,</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#212121;font-style:inherit;--shiki-dark:#EEFFFF;--shiki-dark-font-style:italic">    text</span><span data-fern-style="color:#D32F2F;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">=</span><span data-fern-style="color:#212121;--shiki-dark:#82AAFF">text</span><span data-fern-style="color:#212121;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">,</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#212121;font-style:inherit;--shiki-dark:#EEFFFF;--shiki-dark-font-style:italic">    model_id</span><span data-fern-style="color:#D32F2F;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">=</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&quot;</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#C3E88D">eleven_v4</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&quot;</span><span data-fern-style="color:#212121;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">,</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#212121;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">)</span></span></span></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></pre></div><!--/$-->
<p><strong class="font-semibold">Best practices</strong></p>
<ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class="">Use standard IPA symbols from the <a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Phonetic_Alphabet">International Phonetic Alphabet chart<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a></li>
<li class="">Include stress markers: primary stress (ˈ) and secondary stress (ˌ) for multi-syllable words</li>
<li class="">Apply selectively: only wrap specific words or phrases that need pronunciation control</li>
<li class="">Test with your voice: different voices may interpret IPA slightly differently</li>
</ul>
<p><strong class="font-semibold">Troubleshooting</strong></p>
<!--$--><div class="fern-accordion fern-card m-mdx" data-orientation="vertical"><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="pronunciation-is-still-incorrect"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12R5a6l:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R1a6l:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Pronunciation is still incorrect</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12R5a6l:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R1a6l:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><p>Verify your IPA transcription is accurate using an IPA dictionary. Include stress markers (ˈ for
primary stress, ˌ for secondary stress) for multi-syllable words. Test with different voices as
some may interpret IPA more accurately than others.</p></div></div></details><!--/$--><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="inconsistent-results-with-the-same-ipa"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12R5i6l:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R1i6l:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Inconsistent results with the same IPA</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12R5i6l:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R1i6l:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><p>IPA pronunciation is more consistent than in previous models, but results can still vary by
voice and phrase. We recommend testing important pronunciations with your chosen voice before
relying on them in production. If you need a consistent result, generate more than once and
select the best result.</p></div></div></details><!--/$--></div><!--/$-->
<h4 id="phoneme-tags-for-v2-models" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Phoneme tags for v2 models</h4>
<p>Specify pronunciation using <a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speech_Synthesis_Markup_Language">SSML phoneme tags<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a> with v2 models. Supported alphabets include <a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CMU_Pronouncing_Dictionary">CMU<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a> Arpabet and the <a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Phonetic_Alphabet">International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a>.</p>
<div data-intent="note" class="fern-callout"><div class="flex items-start space-x-4"><div class="[&amp;_svg]:size-icon-md mt-0.5 w-4"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-pin"><path d="M12 17v5"></path><path d="M9 10.76a2 2 0 0 1-1.11 1.79l-1.78.9A2 2 0 0 0 5 15.24V16a1 1 0 0 0 1 1h12a1 1 0 0 0 1-1v-.76a2 2 0 0 0-1.11-1.79l-1.78-.9A2 2 0 0 1 15 10.76V7a1 1 0 0 1 1-1 2 2 0 0 0 0-4H8a2 2 0 0 0 0 4 1 1 0 0 1 1 1z"></path></svg></div><div class="-my-4 flex-1 overflow-x-auto text-sm before:mb-4 before:block after:mt-4 after:block"><div class="font-heading text-base font-semibold leading-snug"></div><p>Phoneme tags are only compatible with the <code>eleven_flash_v2</code> <a data-astro-prefetch="viewport" class="fern-mdx-link" href="/docs/overview/models">model</a>.</p></div></div></div>

<p>We recommend using CMU Arpabet for consistent and predictable results with v2 models. While IPA can be effective, CMU Arpabet generally offers more reliable performance.</p>
<p>Phoneme tags only work for individual words. If you have a name with a first and last name that you want to be pronounced a certain way, you will need to create a phoneme tag for each word.</p>
<p>Ensure correct stress marking for multi-syllable words to maintain accurate pronunciation:</p>

class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#EEFFFF">  pronunciation</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&lt;/</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#F07178">phoneme</span><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&gt;</span></span></span></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></pre></div><!--/$-->
<h4 id="alias-tags" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Alias Tags</h4>
<p>For models that don’t support phoneme tags, you can try writing words more phonetically. You can also employ various tricks such as capital letters, dashes, apostrophes, or even single quotation marks around a single letter or letters.</p>
<p>As an example, a word like “trapezii” could be spelt “trapezIi” to put more emphasis on the “ii” of the word.</p>
<p>You can either replace the word directly in your text, or if you want to specify pronunciation using other words or phrases when using a pronunciation dictionary, you can use alias tags for this. This can be useful if you’re generating using Multilingual v2, which doesn’t support phoneme tags. You can use pronunciation dictionaries with ElevenCreative Studio, Dubbing Studio and Speech Synthesis via the API.</p>
<p>For example, if your text includes a name that has an unusual pronunciation that the AI might struggle with, you could use an alias tag to specify how you would like it to be pronounced:</p>

<p>If you want to make sure that an acronym is always delivered in a certain way whenever it is encountered in your text, you can use an alias tag to specify this:</p>

<h4 id="pronunciation-dictionaries" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Pronunciation Dictionaries</h4>
<p>Some of our tools, such as ElevenCreative Studio and Dubbing Studio, allow you to create and upload a pronunciation dictionary. These allow you to specify the pronunciation of certain words, such as character or brand names, or to specify how acronyms should be read.</p>
<p>Pronunciation dictionaries allow this functionality by enabling you to upload a lexicon or dictionary file that specifies pairs of words and how they should be pronounced, either using a phonetic alphabet or word substitutions.</p>
<p>Whenever one of these words is encountered in a project, the AI model will pronounce the word using the specified replacement.</p>
<p>To provide a pronunciation dictionary file, open the settings for a project and upload a file in either TXT or the <a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://www.w3.org/TR/pronunciation-lexicon/">.PLS format<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a>. When a dictionary is added to a project it will automatically recalculate which pieces of the project will need to be re-converted using the new dictionary file and mark these as unconverted.</p>
<p>Currently we only support pronunciation dictionaries that specify replacements using phoneme or alias tags.</p>
<p>Both phonemes and aliases are sets of rules that specify a word or phrase they are looking for, referred to as a grapheme, and what it will be replaced with. Please note that searches are case sensitive. When checking for a replacement word in a pronunciation dictionary, the dictionary is checked from start to end and only the very first replacement is used.</p>
<h4 id="pronunciation-dictionary-examples" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Pronunciation Dictionary examples</h4>
<p>Here are examples of pronunciation dictionaries in both CMU Arpabet and IPA, including a phoneme to specify the pronunciation of “Apple” and an alias to replace “UN” with “United Nations”:</p>

data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&gt;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">  &lt;/</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#F07178">lexeme</span><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&gt;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&lt;/</span><span data-fern-style="color:#22863A;--shiki-dark:#F07178">lexicon</span><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#89DDFF">&gt;</span></span></span></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></pre></div><!--/$-->
<p>To generate a pronunciation dictionary <code>.pls</code> file, there are a few open source tools available:</p>
<ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class=""><a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://github.com/sequitur-g2p/sequitur-g2p">Sequitur G2P<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a> - Open-source tool that learns pronunciation rules from data and can generate phonetic transcriptions.</li>
<li class=""><a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://github.com/AdolfVonKleist/Phonetisaurus">Phonetisaurus<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a> - Open-source G2P system trained on existing dictionaries like CMUdict.</li>
<li class=""><a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://github.com/espeak-ng/espeak-ng">eSpeak<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a> - Speech synthesizer that can generate phoneme transcriptions from text.</li>
<li class=""><a class="fern-mdx-link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" href="https://github.com/cmusphinx/cmudict">CMU Pronouncing Dictionary<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-external-link external-link-icon"><path d="M15 3h6v6"></path><path d="M10 14 21 3"></path><path d="M18 13v6a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V8a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h6"></path></svg></a> - A pre-built English dictionary with phonetic transcriptions.</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="emotion" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Emotion</h3>
<p>Convey emotions through narrative context or explicit dialogue tags. This approach helps the AI understand the tone and emotion to emulate.</p>
<!--$--><div data-language="text" class="fern-code fern-code-block bg-card-background border-card-border rounded-3 shadow-card-grayscale relative mb-6 mt-4 flex w-full min-w-0 max-w-full flex-col class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>You&#x27;re leaving?&quot; she asked, her voice trembling with sadness. &quot;That&#x27;s it!&quot; he exclaimed triumphantly.</span></span></span></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></pre></div><!--/$-->
<p>Explicit dialogue tags yield more predictable results than relying solely on context, however the model will still speak out the emotional delivery guides. These can be removed in post-production using an audio editor if unwanted.</p>
<h3 id="pace" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Pace</h3>
<p>The pacing of the audio is highly influenced by the audio used to create the voice. When creating your voice, we recommend using longer, continuous samples to avoid pacing issues like unnaturally fast speech.</p>
<p>For control over the speed of the generated audio, you can use the speed setting. This allows you to either speed up or slow down the speed of the generated speech. The speed setting is available in Text to Speech via the website and API, as well as in ElevenCreative Studio and Agents Platform. It can be found in the voice settings.</p>
<p>The default value is 1.0, which means that the speed is not adjusted. Values below 1.0 will slow the voice down, to a minimum of 0.7. Values above 1.0 will speed up the voice, to a maximum of 1.2. Extreme values may affect the quality of the generated speech.</p>
<p>Pacing can also be controlled by writing in a natural, narrative style.</p>
<!--$--><div data-language="text" class="fern-code fern-code-block bg-card-background border-card-border rounded-3 shadow-card-grayscale relative mb-6 mt-4 flex w-full min-w-0 max-w-full flex-col <h3 id="tips" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Tips</h3>
<!--$--><div class="fern-accordion fern-card m-mdx" data-orientation="vertical"><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="common-issues"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12R5agl:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R1agl:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Common Issues</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12R5agl:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R1agl:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center"><li class=""><p>Inconsistent pauses: Ensure <code>&lt;break time=“x.xs” /&gt;</code> syntax is used for
pauses.</p></li><li class="">Pronunciation errors: Use CMU Arpabet or IPA phoneme tags for precise pronunciation.</li><li class=""><div>Emotion mismatch: Add narrative context or explicit tags to guide emotion.<!-- --> <!-- -->
<strong class="font-semibold">Remember to remove any emotional guidance text in post-production.</strong></div></li></ul></div></div></details><!--/$--><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="tips-for-improving-output"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12R5igl:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R1igl:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Tips for Improving Output</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12R5igl:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R1igl:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><p>Experiment with alternative phrasing to achieve desired pacing or emotion. For complex sound
effects, break prompts into smaller, sequential elements and combine results manually.</p></div></div></details><!--/$--></div><!--/$-->
<h3 id="creative-control" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Creative control</h3>
<p>While we are actively developing a “Director’s Mode” to give users even greater control over outputs, here are some interim techniques to maximize creativity and precision:</p>
<!--$--><div class="fern-steps" tocDepth="3"><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="narrative-styling"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#narrative-styling" aria-label="Link to step 1"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>1</span></span></a><h3>Narrative styling</h3><p>Write prompts in a narrative style, similar to scriptwriting, to guide tone and pacing effectively.</p></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="layered-outputs"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#layered-outputs" aria-label="Link to step 2"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>2</span></span></a><h3>Layered outputs</h3><p>Generate sound effects or speech in segments and layer them together using audio editing software for more complex compositions.</p></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="phonetic-experimentation"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#phonetic-experimentation" aria-label="Link to step 3"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>3</span></span></a><h3>Phonetic experimentation</h3><p>If pronunciation isn’t perfect, experiment with alternate spellings or phonetic approximations to achieve desired results.</p></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="manual-adjustments"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#manual-adjustments" aria-label="Link to step 4"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>4</span></span></a><h3>Manual adjustments</h3><p>Combine individual sound effects manually in post-production for sequences that require precise timing.</p></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="feedback-iteration"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#feedback-iteration" aria-label="Link to step 5"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>5</span></span></a><h3>Feedback iteration</h3><p>Iterate on results by tweaking descriptions, tags, or emotional cues.</p></div><!--/$--></div><!--/$-->
<h2 id="text-normalization" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Text normalization</h2>
<p>When using Text to Speech with complex items like phone numbers, zip codes and emails they might be mispronounced. This is often due to the specific items not being in the training set and smaller models failing to generalize how they should be pronounced. This guide will clarify when those discrepancies happen and how to have them pronounced correctly.</p>
<div data-intent="tip" class="fern-callout"><div class="flex items-start space-x-4"><div class="[&amp;_svg]:size-icon-md mt-0.5 w-4"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-star"><path d="M11.525 2.295a.53.53 0 0 1 .95 0l2.31 4.679a2.123 2.123 0 0 0 1.595 1.16l5.166.756a.53.53 0 0 1 .294.904l-3.736 3.638a2.123 2.123 0 0 0-.611 1.878l.882 5.14a.53.53 0 0 1-.771.56l-4.618-2.428a2.122 2.122 0 0 0-1.973 0L6.396 21.01a.53.53 0 0 1-.77-.56l.881-5.139a2.122 2.122 0 0 0-.611-1.879L2.16 9.795a.53.53 0 0 1 .294-.906l5.165-.755a2.122 2.122 0 0 0 1.597-1.16z"></path></svg></div><div class="-my-4 flex-1 overflow-x-auto text-sm before:mb-4 before:block after:mt-4 after:block"><div class="font-heading text-base font-semibold leading-snug"></div><p>Normalization is enabled by default for all TTS models to help improve pronunciation of numbers,
dates, and other complex text elements.</p></div></div></div>
<h3 id="why-do-models-read-out-inputs-differently" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Why do models read out inputs differently?</h3>
<p>Certain models are trained to read out numbers and phrases in a more human way. For instance, the phrase “$1,000,000” is correctly read out as “one million dollars” by the Eleven Multilingual v2 model. However, the same phrase is read out as “one thousand thousand dollars” by the Eleven Flash v2.5 model.</p>
<p>The reason for this is that the Multilingual v2 model is a larger model and can better generalize the reading out of numbers in a way that is more natural for human listeners, whereas the Flash v2.5 model is a much smaller model and so cannot.</p>
<h4 id="common-examples" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Common examples</h4>
<p>Text to Speech models can struggle with the following:</p>
<ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class="">Phone numbers (“123-456-7890”)</li>
<li class="">Currencies (“$47,345.67”)</li>
<li class="">Calendar events (“2024-01-01”)</li>
<li class="">Time (“9:23 AM”)</li>
<li class="">Addresses (“123 Main St, Anytown, USA”)</li>
<li class="">URLs (“example.com/link/to/resource”)</li>
<li class="">Abbreviations for units (“TB” instead of “Terabyte”)</li>
<li class="">Shortcuts (“Ctrl + Z”)</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="mitigation" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Mitigation</h3>
<h4 id="use-trained-models" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Use trained models</h4>
<p>The simplest way to mitigate this is to use a TTS model that is trained to read out numbers and phrases in a more human way, such as the Eleven Multilingual v2 model. This however might not always be possible, for instance if you have a use case where low latency is critical (e.g. conversational agents).</p>
<h4 id="apply-normalization-in-llm-prompts" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Apply normalization in LLM prompts</h4>
<p>In the case of using an LLM to generate the text for TTS, you can add normalization instructions to the prompt.</p>
<!--$--><div class="fern-steps"><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="use-clear-and-explicit-prompts"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#use-clear-and-explicit-prompts" aria-label="Link to step 1"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>1</span></span></a><h3>Use clear and explicit prompts</h3><p>LLMs respond best to structured and explicit instructions. Your prompt should clearly specify that you want text converted into a readable format for speech.</p></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="handle-different-number-formats"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#handle-different-number-formats" aria-label="Link to step 2"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>2</span></span></a><h3>Handle different number formats</h3><p>Not all numbers are read out in the same way. Consider how different number types should be spoken:</p><ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class="">Cardinal numbers: 123 → “one hundred twenty-three”</li>
<li class="">Ordinal numbers: 2nd → “second”</li>
<li class="">Monetary values: $45.67 → “forty-five dollars and sixty-seven cents”</li>
<li class="">Phone numbers: “123-456-7890” → “one two three, four five six, seven eight nine zero”</li>
<li class="">Decimals &amp; Fractions: “3.5” → “three point five”, “⅔” → “two-thirds”</li>
<li class="">Roman numerals: “XIV” → “fourteen” (or “the fourteenth” if a title)</li>
</ul></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="remove-or-expand-abbreviations"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#remove-or-expand-abbreviations" aria-label="Link to step 3"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>3</span></span></a><h3>Remove or expand abbreviations</h3><p>Common abbreviations should be expanded for clarity:</p><ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class="">“Dr.” → “Doctor”</li>
<li class="">“Ave.” → “Avenue”</li>
<li class="">“St.” → “Street” (but “St. Patrick” should remain)</li>
</ul><p>You can request explicit expansion in your prompt:</p><blockquote>
<p>Expand all abbreviations to their full spoken forms.</p>
</blockquote></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="alphanumeric-normalization"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#alphanumeric-normalization" aria-label="Link to step 4"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>4</span></span></a><h3>Alphanumeric normalization</h3><p>Not all normalization is about numbers, certain alphanumeric phrases should also be normalized for clarity:</p><ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class="">Shortcuts: “Ctrl + Z” → “control z”</li>
<li class="">Abbreviations for units: “100km” → “one hundred kilometers”</li>
<li class="">Symbols: “100%” → “one hundred percent”</li>
<li class="">URLs: “elevenlabs.io/docs” → “eleven labs dot io slash docs”</li>
<li class="">Calendar events: “2024-01-01” → “January first, two-thousand twenty-four”</li>
</ul></div><!--/$--><!--$--><div class="fern-step" id="consider-edge-cases"><a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-anchor" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#consider-edge-cases" aria-label="Link to step 5"><span class="fern-anchor-icon"><span>5</span></span></a><h3>Consider edge cases</h3><p>Different contexts might require different conversions:</p><ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class="">Dates: “01/02/2023” → “January second, twenty twenty-three” or “the first of February, twenty twenty-three” (depending on locale)</li>
<li class="">Time: “14:30” → “two thirty PM”</li>
</ul><p>If you need a specific format, explicitly state it in the prompt.</p></div><!--/$--></div><!--/$-->
<h5 id="putting-it-all-together" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Putting it all together</h5>
<p>This prompt will act as a good starting point for most use cases:</p>
<!--$--><div data-language="text" class="fern-code-block not-prose bg-card-background border-card-border rounded-3 shadow-card-grayscale group relative mb-6 mt-4 flex w-full border language-text"><pre class="code-block-root not-prose" data-fern-style="background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#212121;color:#24292eff;--shiki-dark:#EEFFFF" tabindex="0" data-language="txt" data-code-highlighted="true"><div dir="ltr" class="fern-scroll-area" data-fern-style="position:relative;--radix-scroll-area-corner-width:0px;--radix-scroll-area-corner-height:0px"><div data-radix-scroll-area-viewport="" class="fern-scroll-area-viewport" data-scrollbars="both" data-fern-style="overflow-x:hidden;overflow-y:hidden"><div data-fern-style="min-width:100%;display:table"><div class="code-block text-sm"><div class="code-block-inner"><div class="code-block-line-group code-block-flow"><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>Convert the output text into a format suitable for text-to-speech. <h4 id="use-regular-expressions-for-preprocessing" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Use Regular Expressions for preprocessing</h4>
<p>If using code to prompt an LLM, you can use regular expressions to normalize the text before providing it to the model. This is a more advanced technique and requires some knowledge of regular expressions. Here are some simple examples:</p>

number-to-word conversion</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span data-fern-style="color:#24292EFF;--shiki-dark:#EEFFFF">p <h2 id="prompting-eleven-v4" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Prompting Eleven v4</h2>
<p>This section is written for Eleven v4. Many of the techniques below also apply to <a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-mdx-link" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#prompting-eleven-v3">Eleven v3</a>. In general, Eleven v4 is a net upgrade over Eleven v3, delivering better results in almost every case. We strongly recommend switching to v4 and testing it with your own voices and content to see the difference for yourself. While a few edge cases may call for a different fit, most users should find v4 the better choice.</p>
<p>For what the model changes — voice cloning, accent handling, variants, and comparisons — see <a data-astro-prefetch="viewport" class="fern-mdx-link" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/eleven-v4">Eleven v4</a>.</p>
<div data-intent="info" class="fern-callout"><div class="flex items-start space-x-4"><div class="[&amp;_svg]:size-icon-md mt-0.5 w-4"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-info"><circle cx="12" cy="12" r="10"></circle><path d="M12 16v-4"></path><path d="M12 8h.01"></path></svg></div><div class="-my-4 flex-1 overflow-x-auto text-sm before:mb-4 before:block after:mt-4 after:block"><div class="font-heading text-base font-semibold leading-snug"></div><p>Eleven v4 and Eleven v3 do not support SSML break tags. Use audio tags, punctuation (ellipses),
and text structure to control pauses and pacing.</p></div></div></div>
<h3 id="voice-selection" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Voice selection</h3>
<p>The voice still matters. A delivery that is already in the training data — whispering, shouting, a particular delivery — is easier for the model to reproduce. Asking for something outside that data is harder. Eleven v4 follows audio tags more reliably than earlier models, including when the voice wasn’t trained on that delivery. A voice that has never whispered should still be able to follow <code>[whispering]</code>, and a voice that has never shouted should still be able to follow <code>[shouting]</code>. However, it might be less reliable, and the result may not be optimal. From early testing, it seems to work quite well. We strongly recommend testing it yourself with the voice you want and for your specific use case.</p>
<h3 id="audio-tags" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Audio tags</h3>
<p>Audio tags (e.g. <code>[whispering]</code>, <code>[shouting]</code>, <code>[laughing]</code>) let you direct delivery with fine-grained control, and Eleven v4 handles them with a level of nuance beyond previous models. They’re not perfect yet, and we’re continuing to iterate and improve how reliably the model follows tag instructions — this is an active area of ongoing investment, and it will keep getting better.</p>
<p>Being explicit about what you want helps a lot. Because Eleven v4 is trained to generate both vocal delivery styles and sound effects, a tag can occasionally be interpreted as a request for a sound effect rather than a delivery instruction (or vice versa). Writing tags that clearly describe the voice quality you want (e.g. <code>[low, gravelly voice]</code> rather than something that could be read as a sound cue) helps the model deliver what you intended. We recommend testing your specific tags and phrasing for your use case, and expect this to keep improving.</p>
<div data-intent="note" class="fern-callout"><div class="flex items-start space-x-4"><div class="[&amp;_svg]:size-icon-md mt-0.5 w-4"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-pin"><path d="M12 17v5"></path><path d="M9 10.76a2 2 0 0 1-1.11 1.79l-1.78.9A2 2 0 0 0 5 15.24V16a1 1 0 0 0 1 1h12a1 1 0 0 0 1-1v-.76a2 2 0 0 0-1.11-1.79l-1.78-.9A2 2 0 0 1 15 10.76V7a1 1 0 0 1 1-1 2 2 0 0 0 0-4H8a2 2 0 0 0 0 4 1 1 0 0 1 1 1z"></path></svg></div><div class="-my-4 flex-1 overflow-x-auto text-sm before:mb-4 before:block after:mt-4 after:block"><div class="font-heading text-base font-semibold leading-snug"></div><p>Tags land more readily when the delivery is already in the voice’s training data. Eleven v4 can
still follow a tag the voice was not trained on, such as <code>[whispering]</code> or <code>[shouting]</code>, though
the result may not be optimal.</p></div></div></div>
<h4 id="voice-related" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Voice-related</h4>
<p>These tags control vocal delivery and emotional expression:</p>
<ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class=""><code>[laughs]</code>, <code>[laughs harder]</code>, <code>[starts laughing]</code>, <code>[wheezing]</code></li>
<li class=""><code>[whispers]</code></li>
<li class=""><code>[sighs]</code>, <code>[exhales]</code></li>
<li class=""><code>[sarcastic]</code>, <code>[curious]</code>, <code>[excited]</code>, <code>[crying]</code>, <code>[snorts]</code>, <code>[mischievously]</code></li>
</ul>

<h4 id="sound-effects" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Sound effects</h4>
<p>Add environmental sounds and effects:</p>
<ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">

<li class=""><code>[swallows]</code>, <code>[gulps]</code></li>
</ul>

<h4 id="unique-and-special" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Unique and special</h4>
<p>Experimental tags for creative applications:</p>
<ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class=""><code>[strong X accent]</code> (replace X with desired accent)</li>
<li class=""><code>[sings]</code>, <code>[woo]</code>, <code>[fart]</code></li>
</ul>

<h3 id="punctuation" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Punctuation</h3>
<p>Punctuation significantly affects delivery in v4:</p>
<ul class="[&amp;&gt;li]:relative [&amp;&gt;li]:before:text-(color:--grayscale-a10) mb-3 list-none pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:pl-3 [&amp;&gt;li]:before:absolute [&amp;&gt;li]:before:ml-[-22px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:mt-[-1px] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:content-[&#x27;⦁&#x27;] [&amp;&gt;li]:before:self-center">
<li class=""><strong class="font-semibold">Ellipses (…)</strong> add pauses and weight</li>
<li class=""><strong class="font-semibold">Capitalization</strong> increases emphasis</li>
<li class=""><strong class="font-semibold">Standard punctuation</strong> provides natural speech rhythm</li>
</ul>

<h3 id="single-speaker-examples" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Single speaker examples</h3>
<p>Use tags intentionally and match them to the voice’s character. A meditative voice shouldn’t shout; a hyped voice won’t whisper convincingly.</p>

<h3 id="multi-speaker-dialogue" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Multi-speaker dialogue</h3>
<p>v4 can handle multi-voice prompts effectively. Assign distinct voices from your Voice Library for each speaker to create realistic conversations.</p>
<!--$--><div dir="ltr" data-orientation="horizontal"><div class="border-border-default mb-6 mt-4 flex items-end border-b first:-mt-3"><div class="fern-x-overflow flex min-w-0 flex-1 gap-4"><h6 class="text-(color:--grayscale-a11) hover:border-border-default -mb-px flex max-w-max cursor-pointer whitespace-nowrap border-b border-transparent pb-2.5 pt-3 text-sm font-semibold leading-6 <h3 id="enhancing-input" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Enhancing input</h3>
<p>In the ElevenLabs UI, you can automatically generate relevant audio tags for your input text by clicking the “Enhance” button. Behind the scenes this uses an LLM to enhance your input text with the following prompt:</p>
<!--$--><div data-language="text" class="fern-code-block not-prose bg-card-background border-card-border rounded-3 shadow-card-grayscale group relative mb-6 mt-4 flex w-full border language-text"><pre class="code-block-root not-prose" data-fern-style="background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#212121;color:#24292eff;--shiki-dark:#EEFFFF" tabindex="0" data-language="txt" data-code-highlighted="true"><div dir="ltr" class="fern-scroll-area" data-fern-style="position:relative;--radix-scroll-area-corner-width:0px;--radix-scroll-area-corner-height:0px"><div data-radix-scroll-area-viewport="" class="fern-scroll-area-viewport" data-scrollbars="both" data-fern-style="overflow-x:hidden;overflow-y:hidden;max-height:479px"><div data-fern-style="min-width:100%;display:table"><div class="code-block text-sm"><div class="code-block-inner"><div class="code-block-line-group code-block-flow"><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span># Instructions</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>## 1. Role and Goal</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>You are an AI assistant specializing in enhancing dialogue text for speech generation.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>Your **PRIMARY GOAL** is to dynamically integrate **audio tags** (e.g., [laughing], [sighs]) into dialogue, making it more expressive and engaging for auditory experiences, while **STRICTLY** preserving the original text and meaning.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>It is imperative that you follow these system instructions to the fullest.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>## 2. Core Directives</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>Follow these directives meticulously to ensure high-quality output.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>### Positive Imperatives (DO):</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO integrate **audio tags** from the &quot;Audio Tags&quot; list (or similar contextually appropriate **audio tags**) to add expression, emotion, and realism to the dialogue. These tags MUST describe something auditory.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO ensure that all **audio tags** are contextually appropriate and genuinely enhance the emotion or subtext of the dialogue line they are associated with.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO strive for a diverse range of emotional expressions (e.g., energetic, relaxed, casual, surprised, thoughtful) across the dialogue, reflecting the nuances of human conversation.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO place **audio tags** strategically to maximize impact, typically immediately before the dialogue segment they modify or immediately after. (e.g., [annoyed] This is hard. or This is hard. [sighs]).</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO ensure **audio tags** contribute to the enjoyment and engagement of spoken dialogue.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>### Negative Imperatives (DO NOT):</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO NOT alter, add, or remove any words from the original dialogue text itself. Your role is to *prepend* **audio tags**, not to *edit* the speech. **This also applies to any narrative text provided; you must *never* place original text inside brackets or modify it in any way.**</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO NOT create **audio tags** from existing narrative descriptions. **Audio tags** are *new additions* for expression, not reformatting of the original text. (e.g., if the text says &quot;He laughed loudly,&quot; do not change it to &quot;[laughing loudly] He laughed.&quot; Instead, add a tag if appropriate, e.g., &quot;He laughed loudly [chuckles].&quot;)</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO NOT use tags such as [standing], [grinning], [pacing], [music].</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO NOT use tags for anything other than the voice such as music or sound effects.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO NOT invent new dialogue lines.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO NOT select **audio tags** that contradict or alter the original meaning or intent of the dialogue.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* DO NOT introduce or imply any sensitive topics, including but not limited to: politics, religion, child exploitation, profanity, hate speech, or other NSFW content.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>## 3. Workflow</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>1. **Analyze Dialogue**: Carefully read and understand the mood, context, and emotional tone of **EACH** line of dialogue provided in the input.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>2. **Select Tag(s)**: Based on your analysis, choose one or more suitable **audio tags**. Ensure they are relevant to the dialogue&#x27;s specific emotions and dynamics.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>3. **Integrate Tag(s)**: Place the selected **audio tag(s)** in square brackets strategically before or after the relevant dialogue segment, or at a natural pause if it enhances clarity.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>4. **Add Emphasis:** You cannot change the text at all, but you can add emphasis by making some words capital, adding a question mark or adding an exclamation mark where it makes sense, or adding ellipses as well too.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>5. **Verify Appropriateness**: Review the enhanced dialogue to confirm:</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>    * The **audio tag** fits naturally.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>    * It enhances meaning without altering it.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>    * It adheres to all Core Directives.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>## 4. Output Format</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* Present ONLY the enhanced dialogue text in a conversational format.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* **Audio tags** **MUST** be enclosed in square brackets (e.g., [laughing]).</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* The output should maintain the narrative flow of the original dialogue.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>## 5. Audio Tags (Non-Exhaustive)</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>Use these as a guide. You can infer similar, contextually appropriate **audio tags**.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Directions:**</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [happy]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [sad]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [excited]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [angry]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [whisper]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [annoyed]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [appalled]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [thoughtful]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [surprised]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* *(and similar emotional/delivery directions)*</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Non-verbal:**</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [laughing]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [chuckles]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [sighs]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [clears throat]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [short pause]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [long pause]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [exhales sharply]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* [inhales deeply]</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>* *(and similar non-verbal sounds)*</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>## 6. Examples of Enhancement</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Input**:</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>&quot;Are you serious? I can&#x27;t believe you did that!&quot;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Enhanced Output**:</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>&quot;[appalled] Are you serious? [sighs] I can&#x27;t believe you did that!&quot;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>---</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Input**:</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>&quot;That&#x27;s amazing, I didn&#x27;t know you could sing!&quot;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Enhanced Output**:</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>&quot;[laughing] That&#x27;s amazing, [singing] I didn&#x27;t know you could sing!&quot;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>---</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Input**:</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>&quot;I guess you&#x27;re right. It&#x27;s just... difficult.&quot;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>**Enhanced Output**:</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>&quot;I guess you&#x27;re right. [sighs] It&#x27;s just... [muttering] difficult.&quot;</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span># Instructions Summary</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span></span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>1. Add audio tags from the audio tags list. These must describe something auditory but only for the voice.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>2. Enhance emphasis without altering meaning or text.</span></span></span></div><div class="code-block-line"><span class="code-block-line-content"><span class="line"><span>3. Reply ONLY with the enhanced text.</span></span></span></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></pre><div class="absolute right-2 top-2 z-20 flex items-center gap-1 opacity-0 backdrop-blur <h3 id="tips-1" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Tips</h3>
<!--$--><div class="fern-accordion fern-card m-mdx" data-orientation="vertical"><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="tag-combinations"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12Rab05:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R2b05:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Tag combinations</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12Rab05:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R2b05:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><p>You can combine multiple audio tags for complex emotional delivery. Experiment with different
combinations to find what works best for your voice.</p></div></div></details><!--/$--><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="voice-matching"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12Raj05:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R2j05:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Voice matching</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12Raj05:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R2j05:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><p>Match tags to your voice’s character and training data. A serious, professional voice may not
respond well to playful tags like <code>[giggles]</code> or <code>[mischievously]</code>.</p></div></div></details><!--/$--><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="text-structure"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12Rar05:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R2r05:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Text structure</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12Rar05:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R2r05:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><p>Text structure strongly influences output with v4. Use natural speech patterns, proper
punctuation, and clear emotional context for best results.</p></div></div></details><!--/$--><!--$--><details data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" class="fern-accordion-item" id="experimentation"><summary type="button" aria-controls="radix-:r12Rb305:" aria-expanded="false" data-state="closed" data-orientation="vertical" id="radix-:r12R3305:" class="fern-accordion-trigger" data-radix-collection-item=""><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-right fern-accordion-trigger-arrow"><path d="m9 18 6-6-6-6"></path></svg><h6 class="fern-accordion-trigger-title">Experimentation</h6></summary><div data-state="closed" id="radix-:r12Rb305:" role="region" aria-labelledby="radix-:r12R3305:" data-orientation="vertical" data-fern-style="--radix-accordion-content-height:var(--radix-collapsible-content-height);--radix-accordion-content-width:var(--radix-collapsible-content-width)"><div class="px-5 pt-3 pb-3"><p>There are likely many more effective tags beyond this list. Experiment with descriptive
emotional states and actions to discover what works for your specific use case.</p></div></div></details><!--/$--></div><!--/$-->

<h2 id="prompting-eleven-v3" class="max-md:pr-4" data-state="closed" data-fern-style="cursor:pointer">Prompting Eleven v3</h2>
<p>The prompting techniques in <a data-astro-prefetch="false" class="fern-mdx-link" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/best-practices#prompting-eleven-v4">Prompting Eleven v4</a> also apply to Eleven v3, including voice selection, audio tags, punctuation, and multi-speaker dialogue.</p>
<p>Professional Voice Clones (PVCs) are not fully optimized for Eleven v3, resulting in potentially lower clone quality compared to earlier models. PVCs are supported in v4, so if you’d like to use a Professional Voice Clone or a voice from the Voice Library, we recommend trying <a data-astro-prefetch="viewport" class="fern-mdx-link" href="/docs/overview/capabilities/text-to-speech/eleven-v4">Eleven v4</a> instead.</p></div><!--/$--><!--astro:end--></astro-island></div></article><div class="grow"></div><div class="contents"><div         (function () {
          // Runs on the initial load AND on every view-transition swap: Astro
          // re-inserts the `transition:persist`ed sidebar into the new document,
          // and re-inserting a scroll container resets its scrollTop to 0, so we
          // restore the position from sessionStorage before paint to avoid a
          // visible jump. The companion scroll listener below keeps that stored
          // value in lockstep with the live scroll (the hydrated build did this
          // via React); without it the restored value drifts stale and the
          // sidebar jumps to the wrong offset on navigation.
          try {
            var KEY = "fern-sidebar-scroll-position";
            var container = document.getElementById("fern-sidebar-scroll-area");
            if (!container) { return; }
            var storedRaw = window.sessionStorage.getItem(KEY);
            var stored = storedRaw != null ? parseInt(storedRaw, 10) : 0;
            if (Number.isFinite(stored) && stored > 0) {
              container.scrollTop = stored;
              return;
            }
            var active = container.querySelector('.fern-sidebar-link[data-state="active"]');
            if (!active) { return; }
            var cRect = container.getBoundingClientRect();
            var aRect = active.getBoundingClientRect();
            var isAbove = aRect.top < cRect.top;
            var isBelow = aRect.bottom > cRect.bottom;
            if (!isAbove && !isBelow) { return; }
            var offset = aRect.top - cRect.top + container.scrollTop;
            var target = Math.max(0, Math.round(offset - container.clientHeight / 3));
            container.scrollTop = target;
            if (target > 0) {
              window.sessionStorage.setItem(KEY, String(target));
            }
          } catch (e) { /* sessionStorage/layout unavailable — fall back to the hydration-time scroll */ }
        })();
      </script></main><!-- Footer (matches AbstractDefaultDocs footer) --><footer id="fern-footer" class="width-before-scroll-bar"></footer></div><div Classic scripts share one global\n                // lexical scope, so order matters: two bundles that both declare\n                // a top-level `const`/`let` of the same name throw \"Identifier\n                // ... has already been declared\" in whichever runs second.\n                // `async = false` runs them in docs.yml order, like next/script.\n                el.async = false;\n                el.setAttribute(\"data-fern-custom-js\", s.src);\n                if (s.integrity) { el.integrity = s.integrity; callback();\n                return;\n              }\n              let done = false;\n              const observer = new MutationObserver(check);\n              const timer = setTimeout(finish,
